### 2026-11-23 (accessibility tree) - Windows UI Automation tree, stable automation ids and live regions; live UIA verified on v1.12.5

**Agent:** lead + `qa-engineer` + `interface-designer` + `dev-ops` (Team mode), on `a11y-v1-12-5` = `origin/master` `9e8029b` (v1.12.5). Note: the first worktree was cut from a stale local `master` `76aba5d` (v1.11.4-era, 161 commits behind); it is discarded and the work was re-ported to v1.12.5.

- **Why:** the owner asked for standard accessibility trees for disabled users, agentic use and automated testing. egui/eframe already writes an AccessKit tree; the gap was that the shell is painted, so most controls had no node, no name or no stable handle.
- **What:** new `client-gui/src/app/a11y.rs` (`AccessKitResponse` helpers, stable `automation` ids, testable `AxTree` + "every input named" invariant) and `client-gui/src/app/a11y_dump.rs` + `lightspeed-gui --a11y-dump <path>`: an offline 11-screen render (main, connected, starved, error, notice, manual, target, settings, editor, picker, update) of the real shell with TEST-NET sample state, no window/tray/network/saved config/autostart probe and no local user profiles, servers or profile keys (the offline app hides them). Labelled: hero state word (polite live), readout detail + metric, route map (Image alt text), data-rail rows, Details (expanded; AccessKit Expand/Collapse consumed, so a screen reader's ExpandCollapsePattern works), location row, starvation banner and command-error panel (Alert/assertive), elevation notice, game-detected line, primary action, relay/game/manual controls, footer, and every sheet title/close/field; `ledger.rs`, `widgets.rs` and `globe.rs` gained role/name/description/expanded metadata. New `scripts/uia-tree.ps1` (live UIA dump + expectations), `docs/accessibility.md`, `docs/README.md` link, `CHANGELOG.md` entry, and two new locale keys (`a11y.route_map`, `a11y.remove_relay`) in all 9 catalogs.
- **QA finding fixed:** the idle map alt text leaked the raw key `route.missing_relay` (the catalogue defines `action_reason.missing_relay`); corrected and asserted.
- **Verification:** `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy -p lightspeed-gui --all-targets` 0 warnings; `cargo test -p lightspeed-gui` **240 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored** (219 baseline + 13 accessibility + 5 dump/CLI + offline-data/coverage + two AccessKit Expand tests); workspace `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude lightspeed-gui` 0 warnings and `cargo test --workspace --all --exclude lightspeed-gui` green (901/0/2 + 715/0/1 + smaller suites; two transient local-port flakes (`a_refused_port_has_no_client_leg`, proxy `test_tcp_unauthenticated_rejected`) passed isolated and on rerun; `gui_boundary` G3 budgets registered the new modules and grown files deliberately); dump audit **0 unnamed input-role nodes and 0 duplicate automation ids** across the 11 screens (44/43/50/49/49/66/67/139/115/89/84 nodes; 56 distinct ids; 54 of 56 constants seeded); live release UIA: 17 ids on the main window and 29 after a UIA `InvokePattern` opened Settings; route and error-panel Details expand/collapse through `ExpandCollapsePattern`; the Game combo popup appears as option nodes and closes; a live `LiveRegionChanged` event (20024) fired for `main.state.word`; all checked ids/roles match the dump; mutation checks (state-word id removed; its live flag removed) fail the state-word test then restore. Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-23/uia/` (live trees, comparison, transcripts) and `wat/evidence/2026-11-23/a11y-labels/` (final dump, audit, gates).
- **Landing:** PR [#226](https://github.com/ShibbityShwab/lightspeed/pull/226) squash-merged to `master` as `95bdd2f` on 2026-11-23 (owner sign-off; all required checks green, including **Linux GUI Build**, **macOS GUI Build**, **Windows GUI Build**, **Headless GUI Smoke**; the post-merge `master` CI run 45152101435 is green too). ### 2026-11-23 (Release 1.12.5 candidate) - pinned driver + true Stop verified on the real driver; release record corrected

**Agent:** lead + `qa-engineer`/qa-verifier (real-driver matrix and control) + `release-scribe` (this record); the owner accepted the control gap.

- **Design** (branch `fix/pinned-driver-1.12.5`, HEAD `84e15c3`): one permanent non-diverting WinDivert handle (filter `"false"`, Network, priority 0, normal flags) is opened before the first diverting handle and held for the app lifetime. A Stop is a true Stop: intercept/inject/shadow close, the hold watcher is cancelled and per-run state is cleared; Optimize reopens only the diverting handles. The pin is released only by explicit app shutdown/Quit (after teardown) or process exit. Bounded `FWP_E_IN_USE` retry applies only with no pin held. Every open/close is logged with a monotonic id + role and counted per kind (pin/intercept/inject/shadow/legacy).
- **Updater** (`84e15c3`): the in-app swap stops the WinDivert service (waits up to 10 s) before copying `WinDivert*.dll`/`.sys` and always relaunches from a `finally`; the GUI refuses an install while the pin is held and shuts the engine down first.
- **Real-driver gate:** **84/84 matrix rows PASS** on `84e15c3` (GUI SHA256 `F8ECE5824D674B186D2DB6C2D91703858F597B37B8A7FA3F317B4A6936EA8DFC`, clean boot, real loopback relays + traffic): 30/30 Stop→Optimize traffic cycles, 20/20 Quit→relaunch, 10/10 scope changes, 5/5 game restarts, relay A→B→A, TCP-profile Stop, Stop during hold, force-kill active/idle, self-update path; **0** `FWP_E_IN_USE`, 0 failed opens/retries, zero quiet-window relay delta after every Stop. Evidence `wat/evidence/2026-11-23/task-107-matrix-84e15c3v2-20261123-115633/`; report `wat/evidence/2026-11-23/task-107-release-report.md` §9.
- **Accepted gap:** the 1.12.3 failing-before control (tag commit `37ff8d0`) ran the same 30-cycle loop on a clean boot: 30/30 cycles, 0 failure hits; control **5 PASS + 1 BLOCKED** (`wat/evidence/2026-11-23/task-107-control-1123-20261123-120914/`). The owner accepted the gap; 1.12.5 does not claim the pin "fixes the original fault" — the design removes the unload/reload churn path.
- **Release-record corrections** (docs, CHANGELOG, decision log; tags untouched): `v1.12.3` and `v1.12.4` were tagged while their in-tree notes said "release candidate"; 1.12.4's real-driver evidence was four rapid cycles that carried **zero intercepted/tunnelled packets**, so its real-driver claim was not established; the 1.12.1–1.12.4 WAT records were never landed (the older "Current state"/"Status" paragraphs below describe the 2026-11-21 position). Remaining record work is outside this tree: public v1.12.3/v1.12.4 release-body/PR text (Lead/owner), packaging/manifest and Cargo version bump to 1.12.5, tag/publish gate.
- **Release notes:** `docs/release-notes-1.12.5.md`; `CHANGELOG.md` `[1.12.5] - 2026-11-23`; decision entries for the pin/true-Stop design and the accepted gap + record correction. $0.00; no tag, publish, commit or push by this round.

**Verified:** matrix 84/84 PASS and control 5 PASS/1 BLOCKED from the stored summaries; tested binary hash; tag/commit identities (`v1.12.3` -> `37ff8d0`, `v1.12.4` -> `47d8343`, HEAD `84e15c3`); design statements against the code docs and the `9e73987`/`84e15c3` diffs. **Not verified:** the original field fault (unreproduced); a full in-app update from a published 1.12.5 asset (none exists); live real-game/other-host coverage. **Open:** owner tag/publish gate; public release/PR text correction (Lead/owner); packaging and Cargo bump on the release branch (Lead); no commit/push.

### 2026-11-20 (watchdog fix) - Endless relay/direct flap fixed; faulting re-tests now escalate and park direct

**Agent:** `geofeed-diligence` (follow-up) + lead (gates/commit/build). Committed.

- **Bug (client, live-proven):** the no-reply watchdog's chain-close branch ran on `fault == None`, which is returned both for a healthy window and for the first faulting window of a streak. Each faulting re-test therefore logged "the relay is returning this server's traffic again", dropped the fallback chain and reset the retry budget - a relay↔direct flap every ~46.5 s. Evidence: 81 fallbacks today, 236 re-tests with 213 at attempt 1, 135/137 "chain closed" events after a non-healthy window.
- **Fix:** `client-gui/src/app/watchdog.rs` - only a window with no fault may close an open chain (`chain_closes_after_window(window) = window.is_none()`); a faulting re-test escalates the existing budget (30 s -> 120 s -> exhausted, relay recorded as rejecting the server and the session parks direct). Healthy relays unchanged.
- **Verification:** new regression test `a_faulty_window_never_closes_an_open_retry_chain` (fails on old semantics with "NoReplyFromRelay is not proof that the relay recovered", passes after; mutation revert re-fails); final lead gates: fmt clean; workspace clippy 0; workspace **1,811 / 0 / 6**; GUI clippy 0; GUI **260 / 0 / 1**.
- **Release note:** the running GUI (pid 10884) had the pre-fix binary; a release rebuild is required to activate the fix. CHANGELOG `[Unreleased] > Fixed` updated.
- **Residual:** a genuinely starving relay still costs one direct episode (intended safety), now bounded per episode; the underlying relay return-path starvation is a fleet/network issue, not a client bug.

**Agent:** panel `sec-ops` + `net-eng-2` + `architect-2` + lead. Decision doc: `docs/design/server-location-online-lookup-review.md`. Nothing committed.

- **Key finding: GeoJS is just MaxMind GeoLite2 City.** An offline GeoLite2-City build (2026-11-17) reproduced GeoJS exactly on 12 IPs, including `US-VA Manassas 20109` and its `accuracy` radii; MaxMind is right because it ingested G-Core's geofeed. ipwho.is and ip-api returned **Tokyo/JP** (wrong); DB-IP City Lite wrong; ipinfo keyless matched the G-Core /24s but is an account/terms decision.
- **Offline paths:** (a) G-Core operator geofeed build-time (exact, no licence stated -> written permission needed); (b) **MaxMind GeoLite2 City build-time table** (free account; GeoLite EULA + CC BY-SA 4.0 attribution/ShareAlike; <=30 downloads/day, keep-current). Both display-only, never feeding `chooser_point` or ranking; labels must carry estimate wording + radius.
- **No-gate deliverables:** add the caption "free IP databases are unreliable for hosting ranges"; fix the pre-existing privacy-policy gap (GUI update check to `api.github.com` is not in the policy).
- **Other findings:** the visualizer's hop timeline is simulated (random IPs/hardcoded city graph) - only endpoints are GeoJS-geolocated; traceroute paths remain weak (ICMP/TTL filtering observed); GeoLite2 was wrong for an adjacent G-Core /24 (LU vs Amsterdam), so it improves but does not guarantee hosting accuracy.
- **Owner gates:** O1 = approve GeoLite2 City licence acceptance (free, CC BY-SA) and/or pursue G-Core geofeed permission; otherwise stay "unknown" + caption.

### 2026-11-20 (HUD toggle) - Relayed vs direct HUD: no ping win in this window; server re-lock mis-detection bug exposed

**Agent:** `hud-runner` + lead. Nothing committed except the earlier sampler + doc.

- **Toggle experiment:** UIA-invoked Disconnect on the GUI at 11:10:02, sampled HUD every 30 s; clean relayed window (share 73.8-85.3 %) vs clean direct window 11:10:42-11:13:12. Medians: **relayed 290 ms / 0.04 % loss (n=3) vs direct 286 ms / 0.075 % (n=4)** - delta **+4 ms** (relay marginally worse at HUD granularity). This contradicts the earlier same-morning relayed 285-291 vs direct 317-320, so the relay benefit is **session/route dependent**, not a constant; the client's own `relay_app_ms`/`direct_ms` were stale constants and were not used.
- **Mis-detection bug found (new, important):** after re-arming interception, the client logged `Locked game server` then `Locked server stale - re-detecting` and re-locked a **different high-port UDP flow that geo-resolves to AWS sa-east-1 (Sao Paulo)** - hence a "Brazil" label the owner saw. The detection is a system-wide UDP port-range heuristic (`32768-60999`), not process/flow aware, so it can latch onto an unrelated flow. The relay then had share 0 % for that endpoint and the watchdog demoted it to direct while the GUI still showed OPTIMIZING.
- **Resolution:** GUI restarted (pid 20652); it re-locked the real server (`178.107.14.170:36704`) and relay-lax-1 recovered to **healthy share 54.5-88.6 %**. Game untouched.
- **Docs:** `docs/design/relay-server-leg-validation.md` §6 added: the triangle floor is **protocol-blind** - direct ICMP (317 ms) exceeded the game's relayed UDP path (285-291 ms), so ICMP cannot be a valid lower bound for the UDP path; F4's peer must be application-level (HUD/GT-B), and the floor must be validated against HUD data before vetoing relay choices.
- **Next fix:** make game-server detection flow-aware (prefer the process's socket/flow, pin the match server the interceptor saw first, and refuse to re-lock unrelated high-port flows); then continue HUD sampling per session to quantify the real benefit.

### 2026-11-20 (GT-B) - HUD screenshots vs client estimate: LAX relayed 285-291 ms; client figure stale/wrong; LAX does help

**Agent:** lead (sampler + capture) - sampler committed (`infra/scripts/sample-game-hud.ps1`).

- **Method:** the sampler captures the game window every 30 s and appends the client's relay state (`relay`, `server`, reply share, verdict, `direct_ms`, `relay_app_ms`) to `wat/evidence/2026-11-20/hud-samples/index.jsonl`; the agent reads the PNGs and transcribes the HUD's `Ping`/`Rtt` fields. 18 samples / 9 min while relayed through relay-lax-1.
- **Result (relayed, LAX, healthy 25 % share):** in-game HUD **285 ms (10:54), 291 ms (10:58), 285 ms (11:02)**, loss 0.11-0.31 %. Direct-path HUD earlier the same morning: **317-320 ms**. So relayed LAX improves the player-visible RTT by **~30-35 ms** - the owner's "330 -> 280" experience is correct and the earlier "LAX is not carrying / no relay helps" claim was an over-generalization from a 29 %-of-windows starvation stretch.
- **Client estimate is wrong/stale:** `relay_app_ms` sat at **327.5 ms** constant across all 18 samples (window p50 re-published every 10 s), ~40 ms above the HUD. The GUI currently presents this figure as the relayed path; it should be labelled an estimate and/or hidden when stale.
- **Implication:** the game HUD is the GT-B peer E1 needs (application-level, works when ICMP is filtered). Next: sample HUD during relayed *and* direct windows to quantify the benefit, and fix the GUI's relayed-path figure (or replace it with the HUD-independent measured S when available).

### 2026-11-20 (E1 fix) - lax prober fixed and proven usable; E1 still n=0 (client quit, relay starving)

**Agent:** `e1-fix` + lead. Nothing committed.

- **Sysctl applied to relay-lax-1 only (canary pattern, run 44664274403):** before `1 0` + `Permission denied`; after `61184 65519`, drop-in persisted, no restart (uptime monotonic). Unprivileged ICMP from GID 61184 answered 1.0-1.2 ms.
- **Deployed prober now works:** relay-lax-1 `/metrics` went from **246/246 bursts unavailable** to **339 bursts / 1,017 probes / 257 unavailable**, with **82 bursts and 246 echoes since the fix and 0 new unavailable**. The earlier "0 usable" was the missing host sysctl, not the code. Relay<->live-server ICMP: **120/120 echoes at ~69.6 ms** in window 3.
- **E1 still not evaluable:** the client `SERVER_LEG_APP` stream stopped at 02:32:31Z because the relay's return path was starving (`no_reply_from_relay`, share 0 %, direct fallback - the relay's own ICMP kept succeeding), and window 3 (run 44665081710, 24 rounds, 120/120 echoes) was empty because the **client quit at 03:05:51Z** 8 s before it started (owner action). n=0; no floor/passive medians. Harvest code was validated by reproducing E1's numbers (n=61, floor 73.2, passive 14.5) on the earlier window, so this is an empty window, not a parser miss.
- **Findings:** (a) R2 rollout needs the `ping_group_range` drop-in on **every** relay that runs the prober; (b) relay-side ICMP success does **not** imply relayed UDP carry (ICMP 120/120 while relayed UDP had 0 replies for some destinations) - the carry record remains the stronger signal; (c) the passive app-RTT peer stays demoted (structural bias shown by E1); (d) F4 floor tolerance still needs calibration (~20-24 ms client-side inconsistency observed).

### 2026-11-20 (E1 result) - n=61 gate met; design FAIL; relay prober dead on lax (missing sysctl), passive peer structurally biased

**Agent:** `e1-live` + lead. Nothing committed.

- **Window:** measurement-only run 44660852157 (02:27-02:48Z), relay-lax-1, 20/20 rounds, 100/100 echoes, S_icmp median **53.3 ms**, no anycast reply. Client on relay-lax-1 (pinned after the auto/SYD issue), 65 `SERVER_LEG_APP` lines in window (all samples >= 1), **n = 61 matched epochs** (gate n >= 30 satisfied).
- **Verdict: FAIL.** Check A (S_icmp >= direct - L1 - 3 ms): **4/61 = 6.6%** (gate >= 90%). Check B (|S_icmp - passive|/passive <= 20%): **0/61** (gate >= 80%). Medians: floor 73.2, S_icmp 53.2, passive **14.5**, Delta(S-floor) -20.0.
- **Root causes (evidence):** (1) **the deployed relay prober measured nothing**: 220 bursts / 660 probes / 220 unavailable, 0 usable, while root ping from the same host answered 100/100 - relay-lax-1 never received `net.ipv4.ping_group_range` (only relay-syd-1 was canaried), so the unprivileged ICMP socket was EPERM. (2) **passive app-RTT is not a valid peer**: 14.5 ms vs 53.2 ms from the same relay (3.7x), because forward->first-response is not an RTT on a busy flow; an earlier destination agreed (141.9 vs 142.0), so the bias is RTT/flow dependent. (3) **the client floor is internally inconsistent** by ~20-24 ms: L1 + L2 = 262.0 < L3 = 285.8, so check A's -3 ms tolerance is too tight; the Delta is that inconsistency, not an implausibly short relay leg. (4) app lines only flowed 02:26:52-02:32:31 before re-locks and 36 `no_reply_from_relay` windows ended the supply.
- **Implications:** R2 rollout must apply the sysctl on every relay that runs the prober (not just the canary); the F4 peer must be the relay's own ICMP S (passive sample demoted to a display-only approximation); the floor check needs a tolerance calibrated to the observed client-side inconsistency (or better paired sampling). The product ranking already treats S as display-only, so no ranking change is implied by this FAIL.
- **Cleanup:** temp branch/worktree and `SERVER_LEG_TARGETS` deleted; no deploy (lax uptime monotonic); GUI (pid 17932) left running; target IPs absent from logs; only `docs/publishing-channels.md` dirty.
- **Next:** apply `ping_group_range` to relay-lax-1 (canary pattern, reversible), confirm `server_leg_probes` usable, re-run a window while the game is live, then re-apply the revised gate.

### 2026-11-19 (panel execution) - TCP option E landed; E1 pilot INCONCLUSIVE (n=2); quiet-flow sampling is the real bottleneck

**Agent:** `rust-dev-5` (TCP option E) + `canary-runner-2` (E1 pilot) + lead (final gates). Nothing committed.

- **TCP backpressure fixed with option E.** `proxy/src/relay.rs`: `TCP_CMD_QUEUE_SLOTS=64`, `TCP_CMD_QUEUE_BYTES=256 KiB`, `TcpCmd::Data(Vec<Bytes>)` batching; per-connection slot/byte reservation **before** `on_segment` (pre-ACK drop = RTO-recoverable), `try_send`, FIN-full tears down only that connection, and `write_all` polls `cancel` with a 60 s stall reset. `metrics.rs`: `DropReason::TcpQueueFull` + Prometheus series. Four new tests (stalled upstream does not block other sessions; pre-ACK drop is retransmit-recoverable; FIN under congestion is per-connection; clean stream zero drops) and mutations: restoring the blocking send fails test (a) at 57.5 ms; moving the gate after `on_segment` fails test (b) with exactly 64 corrupt bytes ACKed. Residual: byte cap is a bound not a fence; out-of-order segments in a full queue are refused (RTO recoverable); watchdog broadens to "any single write blocked 60 s"; new counter is Prometheus-only (health JSON/collector not wired).
- **E1 pilot INCONCLUSIVE.** Client had already quit; relaunched it (now pid 20724, relay-lax-1, same server). Measurement-only dispatches (no deploy, secret-held target, aggregate logs, cleaned up) produced n=2 matched pairs: L3 311-324 ms, L1 227-239 ms, L2 84-84.8 ms, S_icmp 60.3-60.4 ms (450/450 echoes, no sub-ms anycast reply in 2,700 echoes), Delta **+23.6/+24.4 ms (+39-40 %)**, median +24.0, MAD 0.37 - outside band, but n=2 so **INCONCLUSIVE** (gate needs >=8, pre-registration >=30). Tier C (no cloud/hosting match, traceroute provenance not measurable from here).
- **The real finding: quiet-flow L3 sampling is the bottleneck.** Only ~5 accepted samples at session start then ~1 per 7 min; `relay_app_ms` is a window p50 re-published every 10 s and may be minutes stale, so 10 s ticks are pseudo-replicates (n cannot be inflated by ticks). Any F4/E1 design that relies on the shipped INFO log cannot reach 30 epochs; the client must emit per-sample accepted L3 timestamps + RTTs (debug-level sampler or a temporary log line) before E1 is runnable.
- **Gates re-run by the lead on the final tree:** fmt clean; workspace clippy 0 warnings; workspace tests **1,687 / 0 / 6**; `cargo test -p lightspeed-proxy --features quic --lib` **193 / 0 / 0**; GUI clippy exit 0 (known resource-compiler warning); GUI tests **203 / 0 / 1**. HEAD `62c6cf6`, 44 files changed.
- **Next:** emit per-sample L3 timestamps; run E1 over a multi-hour live session (>=30 matched epochs); then apply the gate decision rule; commit remains the owner gate.

### 2026-11-19 (panel) - Three-stream decision: F4 invalid as run; E1 matched-epoch experiment; TCP option E

**Agent:** panel `architect` + `net-eng` + `rust-dev-4` (read-only memos) + lead (synthesis). Decision memo: `docs/design/relay-server-leg-validation.md`. In-flight: `rust-dev-5` (implement option E) and `canary-runner-2` (E1 pilot).

- **F4 as run is invalid.** The canary compared relay-lax ICMP 53.5 ms with a hard-coded `F4_ESTIMATE_MS=73` (no repo derivation; numerically the LAX->Ashburn figure in `server-leg-signal.md §4.4`) from a different window on a degraded link, against a possibly front-ended destination. Cross-window difference of two proxies, not a verdict on the relay leg. **F3 re-scored under shipping rules** (2/3 plausible replies, >=1 ms) is **6/14 = 42.9 %**, not 50 % - so F3 has not passed the production gate either; it proves destination-side filtering and working unprivileged ICMP.
- **Adopted E1 (pre-registered experiment).** >= 60 forwarded destinations across >= 3 relays and >= 2 games; production probe semantics; **>= 30 matched F4 epochs** (>= 3 ICMP bursts + >= 5 fresh L1 samples within +/-30 s of an accepted L3 sample); per-pair `Delta = (L3 - L1_window) - S_icmp`; median/MAD/95 % CI; provenance tiers (tier A = last responsive hop in the hosting ASN; tier B anycast/cloud-global never counts/ranks; two flags to exclude); gates F4 PASS iff |median Delta| <= 20 % (or 3 ms) with >= 80 % in band; F3 PASS iff tier-A per-relay >= 50 % (n >= 30, Wilson); ICMP `S` steers ranking only if both pass, otherwise display-only or bounds/prior. No hard-coded constants; privacy = repo secret targets, aggregate evidence, secret/branch deleted.
- **Adopted TCP option E.** Non-blocking per-connection handoff + pre-reliability drop valve + stall watchdog: gate on a slot/byte budget **before** `on_segment` (a pre-ACK drop is recoverable by the client RTO), batch `TcpCmd::Data(Vec<Bytes>)` via `try_send`, FIN full -> teardown only that connection, writer polls `cancel` and resets after ~60 s no progress. Rejected: `try_send` at the current seam (silent byte corruption after the ack advanced) and wire-level credit control (protocol/client change).
- **Open questions:** do UDP game ports answer TCP SYN; will ordinary play yield >= 30 matched pairs; 3 ms floor for same-region relays; classifier evaluation on the first 60 destinations.

### 2026-11-19 (R2 completion) - TCP tunnel defect fixed; F3/F4 fleet canary measured (F3 pass, F4 fail)

**Agent:** `rust-dev-3` (TCP fix) + `canary-runner` (fleet measurement) + lead (final gates). Nothing committed.

- **TCP tunnel defect (pre-existing) fixed.** Root cause: every data-bearing packet (and FIN) called `take_conn_tx()`, dropping the last mpsc sender and closing `run_tcp_connection`'s channel, so the connection was torn down after its first data packet. Fix: the sender is kept for the connection's life and **cloned** per send; FIN now means "shut the write half, keep reading until the server closes" (as documented); the `None` arm is kept defensively and documented. Regression tests: multi-packet forwarding and FIN-until-server-close, both driving the real `process_tcp_packet` path; mutation check fails 3 tests before the fix and all pass after (plus 30x stress). Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/r2-tcp-conn-tx-*`.
- **F3/F4 fleet canary** (run 44506581242, measurement-only temporary branch, **no proxy deployed**, secret-held targets deleted afterwards, aggregate/redacted logs only): F3 answer rate **7/14 = 50.0 %** on both relay-lax-1 and relay-syd-1 - the same 7 targets answer on both, so the misses are target-side ICMP filtering, not relay failure; this is **PASS but exactly on the >= 50 % gate** (brittle margin). F4 for the live server: relay-lax ICMP **53.5 ms** vs the v1 estimate **73 ms** = **-26.7 %**, outside the 20 % band (**FAIL**); the estimate looks ~20 ms high (possible front-end answering / client-side overhead) and one target is anycast (sub-ms), so the v1-vs-v2 comparison needs re-deriving before it can condemn the relay path. Medians (lax/syd): 53.5/191.0, 57.4/197.0, 53.2/197.0, 64.7/196.0, 143.0/287.0, 141.0/287.0, plus the anycast 0.393/0.719.
- **Final gates re-run by the lead** (HEAD `62c6cf6`, 43 changed files, no temp remote branches, no leftover secret): fmt clean; workspace clippy **0 warnings**; workspace tests **1,682 / 0 / 6**; `cargo test -p lightspeed-proxy --features quic --lib` **188 / 0 / 0**; GUI clippy exit 0 (known no-resource-compiler build warning); GUI tests **203 / 0 / 1**.
- **Residual risk:** TCP backpressure is now reachable - with the channel persistently open a stalled upstream writer can fill the 64-slot mpsc and head-of-line-block the shared inbound loop; mitigation (`try_send` + drop accounting or a per-connection actor) changes forwarding semantics and needs a design decision. F4 comparison is unresolved; F3 margin is thin (broaden destinations/games per G2).
- **Open:** re-derive the F4 client-side comparison; commit; optional prober rollout canary.

### 2026-11-19 (R2 risks) - Linux path proven in CI; meter/floor/TCP-registration closed; new pre-existing TCP defect found

**Agent:** `rust-dev-2` (implementation) + lead (CI Linux check + final gate re-verification). Nothing committed.

- **Linux compile risk closed.** A temporary `ci/linux-check` branch (check-only `deploy.sh`) was dispatched through the existing `Deploy Proxy` workflow; on the ubuntu runner both `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -p lightspeed-proxy --features quic` and `-p lightspeed-client` finished clean (`LINUX_CHECK_OK`, run 44503043141). Branch deleted. The `libc` ICMP path is no longer an unverified risk.
- **Probe egress now metered.** `proxy/src/budget.rs` gains a probe meter (`try_charge_probe`, `metered_total`, `probe_egress_bytes`); `proxy/src/ping.rs` charges 64 B per echo **before** sending, aborts the burst on denial, never panics; relay budget evaluation uses `metered_total()` at the three sites, so probe bytes count toward the ceiling. New Prometheus series `server_leg_probes` / `server_leg_budget_denied`. Tests prove a denied budget sends nothing and a tight budget saturates rather than over-sends.
- **Fail-open floor is now observable.** `ServerLegReport.validated` is true only when a fresh direct ping and client leg made the triangle-floor check actually run; missing inputs still accept the report but mark it unvalidated, and the GUI shows "measured, unvalidated n=…" vs the validated label (new locale key x9; ja/ko/zh-Hans English).
- **TCP sessions now register probe interest** via the same session-token registry (`TcpConnState` counters, shared helper, threshold 20, refresh every 32 forwards); test covers UDP+TCP registration and private-range refusal.
- **Gates re-run by the lead on the final tree** (HEAD `62c6cf6`, 43 changed): fmt clean; workspace clippy **0 warnings**; workspace tests **1,680 / 0 / 6**; `cargo test -p lightspeed-proxy --features quic --lib` **186 / 0 / 0**; GUI clippy exit 0 (known no-resource-compiler warning); GUI tests **203 / 0 / 1**.
- **New pre-existing defect found (not fixed, outside the three findings).** `TcpConnState::take_conn_tx` (proxy/src/relay.rs:613) moves the mpsc sender out on first use and `process_tcp_packet` drops it, so `run_tcp_connection` sees `None` and tears the connection down - a TCP-tunnelled connection likely survives only its first data-bearing packet and may never reach the 20-forward threshold. Forwarding behaviour was deliberately not changed; needs its own multi-packet test + mutation check. Recommend as the next task.
- **Residual risks stated:** probe charges require a metrics collector for the data-plane term; failed-send echoes are still charged 64 B (conservative); `lightspeed_egress_bytes_total` excludes probe bytes; unvalidated reports still feed the "Expected path ~L+S" row (labelled floor). One unrelated redirect-loopback flake failed once early and passed twice afterwards.
- **Open.** Fix the TCP data-path defect; run the F3/F4 canary measurement on relay-syd-1; commit/release remain owner gates.

### 2026-11-19 (R2 review) - Adversarial pass adds the client-capability gate; gates re-verified (1,670 / 0 / 6)

**Agent:** `rust-dev` (review + fixes) + lead (independent re-verification). Nothing committed or pushed.

- **Significant fix: capability-gated push.** R2 could queue unsolicited `MSG_SERVER_LEG` streams to pre-R2 clients (up to their stream limit). Added `ControlMessage::ClientCaps` (tag `0x0B`, 2-byte payload, additive; old relays drop it as unknown), `caps::SERVER_LEG = 0b10`; the client advertises it once per control connection after registration, the relay records it per data-plane token and **only pushes to peers that advertised it**, and `RegisterAck.caps` now advertises relay support (`TCP_TUNNEL | SERVER_LEG`). The proxy push test proves a session with real probe interest but no capability receives nothing.
- **Smaller fixes:** overall `ECHO_TIMEOUT` deadline after each unmatched ICMP reply (a stray host cannot hold the probe thread for seconds); `0.0.0.0/8` refused as "this network" (previously only `0.0.0.0`); the probe registry is `None` without the `quic` feature so a non-control-plane build never probes; the dead `ProbeSkip` enum is removed; the protocol unknown-tag test moved to `0x0C` and the new tags are pinned distinct.

### 2026-11-19 (canary) - R2 Step 0 PASSED on relay-syd-1: unprivileged ICMP works

**Agent:** lead, on the owner's fleet approval and after the owner completed the `gh` device authorization (token now carries `workflow`).

- **Result.** On `relay-syd-1` (`45.76.114.93`) the canary applied `net.ipv4.ping_group_range = 61184 65519` via `/etc/sysctl.d/99-lightspeed-ping.conf`; the unprivileged `SOCK_DGRAM`/`IPPROTO_ICMP` probe run from GID 61184 then returned 3 replies to `1.1.1.1` (**0.82 / 0.76 / 0.58 ms**). Before the drop-in the identical probe failed with `Permission denied`, so the gate is proven both ways. No `CAP_NET_RAW` on the daemon; the sysctl is persisted for R2.
- **How it ran.** Temporary branch `canary/relay-server-leg` dispatched through the existing `Deploy Proxy` workflow, so the GitHub runner used the `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` secret (no key on this host, no new PAT). The branch's `deploy.sh` only uploaded and ran `infra/scripts/relay-server-leg-canary.py` on that one relay - no proxy deploy, no fleet-wide change. Run `44490911461` on commit `0b35a9b`; the branch was deleted afterwards. `gh` scopes are now `gist, read:org, repo, workflow`, obtained through the plain device flow (token stays in the keyring).
- **Fixed after the run.** The script's *privileged comparison* had a missing `socket` import (NameError) and a `struct.pack` arity bug; the unprivileged verdict is printed before that comparison, so the gate result stands, and the script is fixed in the worktree.
- **Verified.** Full log lines and the before/after sysctl values are in `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/fleet-canary/README.md`; only that one relay's sysctl changed, and `--revert` was exercised successfully on the first (buggy) run.
- **Open.** R2 implementation (probe in `proxy/src`, `MSG_SERVER_LEG`, cache/rate gates, client validation, canary F3/F4 counters) is now unblocked but not started. R1 changes remain uncommitted on the worktree; the temporary canary branch is gone.

### 2026-11-19 (R1) - Server-location ladder shipped: cloud regions + hosting networks, honest labels, no Japan

**Agent:** lead, on owner approval ("R1 is approved too"). Implementation + live release-build check.

- **Data.** `infra/scripts/build-server-location.py` builds two committed offline indexes from free, key-less sources: AWS/GCP range feeds -> `client-gui/data/server-cloud.bin` (6,382 ranges / 91 regions) and RIPE Stat announced prefixes for 20 curated hosting ASNs -> `client-gui/data/server-hosting.bin` (16,932 ranges / 20 providers, including AS199524 G-Core); `client-gui/src/server_tables.rs` is generated; `client-gui/data/server-location.sources.json` records sources/hashes. The shipped client makes no network calls to place a server; cost $0.
- **Resolver.** `client-gui/src/server_place.rs`: Cloud (exact provider region, point when known) -> Hosting (network name, **no point**) -> Country (labelled estimate) -> Unknown. 9 tests: AWS us-east-1, GCP europe-west1, the live G-Core server, the DB-IP ASN gap range, unknown addresses, table integrity.
- **Display + chooser.** The route card shows the ladder; the live ROB RUN server now reads `Server location: hosting network — unknown` + `AS199524 · G-Core Labs` (previously "Japan"). The destination-aware chooser now takes a point only from an exact cloud region (`chooser_point`), never a country centroid, so the wrong country cannot steer ranking. 3 new locale keys x9 (ja/ko/zh-Hans keep English because the bundled font subset lacks the extra glyphs).
- **Verified.** `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; GUI **202 / 0 / 1** (was 193; +9 resolver tests); GUI clippy exit 0 with **0 warnings**; release build run live (pid 19384) relaying LAX healthily (reply share 111-138 %, relayed 285 ms vs direct 339 ms) and showing the new labels; screenshot `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/server-location/r1-gui.png`. NOTICE now attributes AWS/GCP ranges and RIPE Stat.
- **Open.** Azure ranges and rDNS are deferred (documented in `docs/design/relay-selection-sota.md`); the map dot is absent for hosting networks by design until a measured server leg exists (R2, blocked on fleet credentials); data regeneration needs network but the committed blobs keep builds offline.

### 2026-11-19 (research) - SOTA survey for relay selection/second-hop measurement; $0 design memo added

**Agent:** lead + three research subagents (`research-latency`, `research-relay`, `research-probing`), on the owner's "spend time researching papers and SOTA … still hit our $0 mark". Read-only; one design memo added (`docs/design/relay-selection-sota.md`), no product code changed.

- **Core finding.** No published method estimates RTT between two arbitrary hosts with zero participation at both ends; every accurate technique consumes a measurement. Network coordinates cannot embed a non-participating game server, and triangle inequality alone has no server-location signal when the client leg is below the direct ping (the floor-tie case just fixed). The missing measurement is L2 (relay→server) for *other* relays.
- **What applies.** Closest SOTA is the WhatsApp joint relay-probing pattern (ANRW'26: both endpoints probe relays; caller-only selection is 24-144 ms worse) and Steam SDR's server-side ping locations - both need control of the server side, which third-party games do not offer. Our transferable stack: relay-vantage probe (S1), relay mesh + bounds (S2), passive labels + learned prior (S3), free metadata ladder (S0).
- **Step-0 unblocked.** The fleet's blocking unknown ("ICMP needs CAP_NET_RAW") is solved without privilege: unprivileged ICMP (`SOCK_DGRAM` + `IPPROTO_ICMP`) with `net.ipv4.ping_group_range = 61184 65519` (systemd DynamicUser UID/GID range) or the common `0 2147483647`; the daemon keeps no `CAP_NET_RAW`. Verify on a canary before writing code.
- **$0 ledger.** All components free: sponsor relays, public cloud range feeds (AWS `ip-ranges.json` + `geo-ip-feed.csv`, GCP/Azure), rDNS geolocation (open-source model), DB-IP fallback, Globalping/RIPE-Atlas free tiers for calibration only (terms-gated; not for probing third-party game servers at scale). No paid database, platform or trial.
- **Rejected:** game-query payload probes (A2S etc.; no-payload policy), `CAP_NET_RAW` on the daemon, geoIP-as-measurement, paid services, anycast (no allocation).
- **Validation plan (V1-V6, all $0):** probe coverage 30-50 servers × 8 relays (>= 50 % gate), ICMP-vs-traceroute divergence (<= 20 %), geo-source scoring, mesh bound width (< 15 ms), policy replay/regret, passive-label stability.
- **Owner gates:** (a) fleet canary for the `ping_group_range` sysctl/probe (host change), (b) G-Core geofeed licence for the last data gap, (c) any use of volunteer platforms against third-party game servers.

- **Free-at-scale filter (verified on this host, $0).** The product path is offline-only. AWS/GCP feeds parse cleanly (AWS uses the legacy `ip_prefix`; a parser reading only `ipv4Prefix` silently misses all of AWS): `3.237.221.191` -> AWS us-east-1, `34.38.12.1`/`34.77.14.49` -> GCP europe-west1. DB-IP ASN Lite (free, 7.1 MB) **misses `178.107.14.0/24`** while RIPE Stat (free, key-less) resolves the IP to AS199524 G-Core and lists the /24 in `announced-prefixes`; the hosting index must therefore be built from a curated ASN list against RIPE Stat at build time, not from DB-IP ASN. Free DNS (Team Cymru) agrees but is validation-only; Globalping/RIPE Atlas free tiers are validation-only, excluded from the product path. Revised R1 = cloud-region index + hosting-provider index + honest "hosting, location unknown" labels, which fixes the observed Japan label with **no licence and no runtime service**.

**Verified:** memo written and internally sourced; free-tier facts spot-checked (Globalping 250 tests/h unauthenticated / 500 registered; AWS `geo-ip-feed.csv` published under RFC 8805; RIPE Atlas 15 credits/connected-minute). Unverified: ICMP answer rate from the relays to real game servers; `ping_group_range` behavior under the deployed unit; rDNS/provider-feed coverage across the 28 games.
**Open:** R1 (S0 metadata ladder + honest unknown) can start with no gate; R2 (relay-vantage probe) needs the owner fleet gate; R4 needs the G-Core geofeed licence decision.

### 2026-11-19 (fix) - WF-081 Phase A: floor tie-break fixed, server dot labelled as an estimate; Phase B blocked on the feed licence

**Agent:** lead, on the owner's "Awesome lets do all of that" after the adversarial review (`wat/evidence/2026-11-19/geo-fix-critique/synthesis.md`). Implements Phase A of that amended plan; supersedes the Phase A owner decision in the 2026-11-19 (diagnosis) entry, while Phase B stays open. Not released; nothing committed.

- **The routing defect was worse than the geodata.** When the direct ping floors several candidates, `GeometricFloor` carries no penalty, so every floored total is exactly the direct ping and the old order fell through to client jitter, then the numerical relay address (`client/src/route/relay_choice.rs`). Live evidence 06:46:30Z: `45.76.114.93:4434 (GeometricFloor, total 325 ms ...) - 4 of 8 candidates raised` - relay-syd-1 won a tie for a Virginia server because its IP sorted first. That happened independently of the DB-IP country error.
- **Fix.** `ScoredRelay` now carries `dest_leg_prior_us` (the destination leg before the floor) and `sort_scored` orders equal totals by it before jitter/address; the prior's magnitude was disproven by the bound but its order is the only destination evidence at a tie. Two tests: the general floored-tie property and a replay of the live inputs (client legs + 325 ms direct + the JP point). Mutation: removing the new key fails exactly those two tests; the restored tree passes.
- **Display honesty.** The route card now states "Server location: country-level estimate from IP" under the map (new `route.location_estimate` key in all nine catalogs); the dot is no longer an unqualified location. The font-subset test forced the English string in ja/ko/zh-Hans (the extra CJK glyphs are not in the bundled face). Visual check on the debug build: window 320x1042, caption under the map, nothing clipped (`wat/evidence/2026-11-19/floor-tie-break/gui-caption.png`); the installed 1.11.3 build was restored afterwards and re-registered on relay-fra.
- **Phase B (geodata) is blocked, with new evidence.** The licensed alternative was checked: DB-IP **City Lite 2026-11** also resolves `178.107.0.0-178.107.17.255` to `JP / Tokyo / Chiyoda City`. Only G-Core's operator geofeed says `US, US-VA, Manassas`, and that feed carries no licence or terms (header: `#AS199524 Geofeed. Was generated at ...`). Shipping derived data needs an explicit owner decision, so the build script and committed table are untouched; the map still plots JP until then.
- **Live observations, corrected after the owner's manual test.** FRA relayed `239 ms` in-game vs `320 ms` direct on the same server (13:53Z window) - a real win. LAX returned no traffic in two automated attempts (`NoReplyFromRelay`, servers `.31`/`.9`), but the owner then selected LAX manually at 07:37Z and it relayed healthily (`1,924 up / 1,758 down`, reply shares 50-135 %, relay RTT 224-303 ms); its in-game ping was `406 ms` against a `410 ms` direct ping - break-even, not better, under the degraded post-outage network (direct was 320 ms earlier). So the earlier no-traffic episodes were the match/server flow state, **not a LAX fault**: LAX is not broken. The **release build** (`cargo build --release -p lightspeed-gui`, 27.4 MB) was then run locally against the live game: caption present in the real window, reply share 93.8 % healthy, relayed quiet-flow **295 ms vs 391 ms direct**, in-game HUD **297 ms** (`wat/evidence/2026-11-19/floor-tie-break/{release-gui.png,release-game.png}`). Both LAX and FRA help by roughly 80-95 ms under their respective network windows, so a same-window A/B is still the only fair comparison. An internet outage at ~14:27Z degraded the path (direct 320 -> 410 ms); the GUI re-registered afterwards. Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/live-ab/{gui-lax-manual.png,game-lax-ping.png}`.
- **State of the machine.** Installed GUI pid 2056, now pinned to LAX by the owner (`auto_select=false`, `selected=207.246.106.36`); the new code exists only in `target/debug` - the installed 1.11.3 build does not carry it until a release, and the map still shows JP because the committed DB-IP table is unchanged (Phase B). `CHANGELOG.md` has an `[Unreleased]` section for the fix; 1.11.4 is the owner gate.

**Verified:** client relay-choice **18/0** (16 + 2 new); mutation 17/2 without the key; GUI **193/0/1**; GUI clippy 0; fmt clean; workspace gate **1,628/0/6** + GUI 193/0/1 (sum of 30 test binaries, `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/floor-tie-break/workspace-gate.txt`). Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/{floor-tie-break,geo-fix-critique,live-ab,route-check}`.
**Open:** owner decision on the G-Core geofeed licence (Phase B; the only source with this range right); owner-gated 1.11.4 for the unreleased fix; the map still says JP; a clean LAX A/B is unmeasured (LAX starved - match-state or a post-update relay issue); the running GUI is the old installed build.

### 2026-11-19 (diagnosis) - Failed DSH session explained; ROB RUN "Tokyo" label traced to a DB-IP country error

**Agent:** lead, on the owner's question ("I have a session that failed out" + "why is lightspeed routing my ROB RUN game to frankfurt and saying the server is in tokyo?"). Read-only: no product code, relay, fleet, release or public post changed.

- **The failed session.** `session-d8a3faf6...` (title "Project audit, GitHub triage, and issue resolution") ended turns 11-13 at 2026-11-18 13:55-14:04Z and turn 14 at 2026-11-19 03:04Z, each with `turn/end reason error: DeepSeek Messages transport failed (code TRANSPORT)` after the 5-retry LLM policy; the transcript is intact (7,899 events, 19.3 MB raw), resumable from the session list, and a fresh session works. The failure is in the DSH -> provider transport, not the repo or relays. The session left `docs/publishing-channels.md` uncommitted (Scoop token/bucket update); **not committed or verified** - its token claims need reconciling with the state block before use.
- **The route.** Live GUI pid 1360: FRA was chosen at 03:02:37Z for the first locked server of the match, `34.77.14.49` (DB-IP BE - a correct FRA pick), and the interceptor restarted onto it. ROB RUN then changed the match server to `178.107.13.65` / `178.107.14.201`; the destination-aware chooser said relay-nrt `167.179.77.127` was better (RegionPrior 156 ms) but kept the live session on FRA ("applies next session"), the designed no-mid-match-hop rule. A second ranking at 03:11:58Z (after the direct ping landed) still put NRT first (`GeometricFloor, total 284 ms, not better than direct (+0 ms, direct 284)`, 3 of 8 candidates floored) and kept the "applies next session" verdict, so the next session would switch to NRT. The server is plotted in Japan because the committed DB-IP Lite table maps `178.107.0.0-178.107.17.255` to JP; **G-Core's own geofeed says `178.107.13.0/24` and `.14.0/24` are US, US-VA, Manassas**, and DB-IP's newest 2026-11 drop still says JP. Ping from this host: server 282-294 ms, NRT 121-130 ms, FRA 200-201 ms, 1.1.1.1 14-21 ms - a Tokyo server would be ~120 ms direct, not ~285 ms. The live FRA path is 284 ms vs 281-284 direct (break-even, at the physical floor); measured client legs (TCP, n=5) are LAX 219, EWR 307, MAD 197, FRA 202, NRT 122, BOM 97, SGP 44, SYD 137 ms, so LAX's model whole path is ~292 ms and SGP's prior is ~380 ms - none is measured better than the FRA path already at direct.
- **Open (owner decision):** (a) operator-geofeed overlay in `infra/scripts/build-geo-ipv4.py` + attribution/privacy update (accurate; adds a second source), (b) code-only prior-veto when the measured direct ping contradicts the country prior (refuses the prior, cannot rank), or (c) document the DB-IP hosting-range limitation. Follow-up measurement changes the advice: with the country corrected to US-VA, the chooser's prior ranks **relay-lax-1 first** (~315 ms) while the measured FRA path is 284 ms and LAX models ~290 ms - the prior charges FRA->Virginia ~131 ms against ~84 ms measured - so the geofeed fix **alone** would move the session to LAX on a worse estimate. Pair it with a measured-server-leg signal (v2 probe) or a prior/measurement consistency guard. User-level workaround until then: pin a relay by name (sets `auto_select=false`, stops the next-session switch); pinning FRA keeps the measured 284 ms path.

**Verified:** transcript `turn/end` records; live GUI log lines; the committed geo table and the operator geofeed; 5-ping medians; the latest DB-IP drop. Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-19/route-check/` (`README.md` + logs/screenshots). Not verified: any provider cause beyond the recorded TRANSPORT code; the failed session's uncommitted SCOOP work.

### 2026-11-18 (team) - WF-076: #178 fixes - control-plane MTU cap and off-thread game detection

**Agent:** lead + `rust-dev` (task-20), `rust-dev-2` (task-23), `qa-engineer` (tasks 21/24), `interface-designer` (task-22 triage, task-25 post-fix check), on the fresh #178 report and the 1.11.1 roll-out check.

- **Trigger.** A 1.11.1 user (Win11 25H2, RTX 3050 + Intel hybrid) reported 1-3 s freezes when moving the window or clicking any button. The trace also still showed `sendmsg error ... 10040 ... len: 1472` toward the relay's QUIC control port. `interface-designer` triaged the report read-only (task-22): **H1** - game auto-detection ran `tasklist` plus a PowerShell `Get-CimInstance` query on the egui thread every 5 s (measured 0.43-0.47 s here, 2.5-3.4 s in the reporter's trace); **H2** - a wgpu present-path/hybrid-GPU contribution is plausible but unconfirmed. The 10040 was a separate, definite bug.
- **Fix 1 - QUIC control-plane MTU (`rust-dev`, task-20).** `build_client_config` hard-coded quinn's DPLPMTUD `upper_bound` to `HARD_CAP_UDP_PAYLOAD` (1472) and ignored the effective `path_mtu_cap`. New pure helper `quic_udp_payload_upper_bound(path_mtu)` = `path_mtu - 28` clamped to `[1200, 1472]`; the supervised control connection takes the cap from engine `self.path_mtu_cap`, main's resolved `cli_mtu_cap`, reroute, or `CONSERVATIVE_PATH_MTU` in the verify-game diagnostic. Explicit 1500 -> 1472 unchanged; **default 1492 -> 1464** (8 bytes lower, matching the data plane and strictly safer); 1400 -> 1372. Public registration fns gained an explicit cap parameter; four integration tests updated with `HARD_CAP_PATH_MTU`; an 8-line `test_support` re-export was added (accepted). +4 tests (helper in lib+bin + supervisor wiring); mutation to always-1472 fails at 1492 (left 1472, right 1464), restore passes.
- **Fix 2 - game detection off the egui thread (`rust-dev-2`, task-23).** New `client-gui/src/app/presence.rs`: one dedicated `GamePresence` worker with a tested `PresenceSchedule` (5 s start-to-start, one scan in flight, no catch-up burst), mpsc result/command channels, `shutdown()` joins (also on Drop, and from `shutdown_engine`), and `presence_scan_allowed` gate = Auto only. `poll_game_presence` now only enables the worker and drains the newest result; the synchronous `try_auto_detect_game()` in `new()` is gone. Manual/Custom no longer scans at all (the old guard let `game_manual` through). +7 tests; both mutations fail (manual gate and worker-enabled path) and restore passes.
- **Verification.** QA task-21 and task-24 both PASS on the same combined tree: helper boundaries re-derived, only one `upper_bound` call site, cap threaded from the real sources, quic-off stub compiles, no egui-thread detection call site, worker lifecycle/cadence/join proven, no test weakened or removed. `interface-designer` task-25 PASS: shell unchanged at 320 px; a synthetic detectable process was auto-detected and applied; Manual mode spawned **0** `tasklist`/`powershell` in 15 s while Auto spawned one pair per 5.06 s, never overlapping; UI jitter <=0.27 s over 30 s; clean quit. Lead final gate: fmt clean, workspace clippy 0 warnings, **1,600 / 0 / 6** workspace (client 1,328 + protocol 86 + proxy 186), GUI clippy 0, GUI **193 / 0 / 1** (186 + 7 presence tests).
- **Not verified.** The reporter's EDR-slowed 2.5-3.4 s freeze and a real sub-1500 path are not reproducible here; H2 (wgpu/hybrid present path) needs the reporter's A/B data. No default-renderer change on one report. The fixes are **unreleased**; a 1.11.2 patch release is the owner's gate.

**Verified:** gate counts above; QA PASS both tasks; visual PASS; mutation fail/restore records; evidence paths in the current-state block. CI on `2556c93`: **14/14 jobs green** (Windows Build & Test, Windows GUI Build, Check & Test, QUIC Integration Tests, Infra Script Tests, Localization, Code Coverage, feature matrices, E2E, smoke); **Security Audit green** on the same commit. Deploy Proxy was running at report time (version-keyed; the release is unchanged at 1.11.1).
**Open:** Deploy Proxy/CodeQL completion; next patch release (owner gate); #178 reporter retest; #168 retest; M1 trend; H2 needs data; `SCOOP_TOKEN`/winget; backlog.

### 2026-11-18 (release) - WF-074: 1.11.1 published, channels completed, release-gate bug fixed

**Agent:** lead, on owner approval ("Yes - tag v1.11.1 and publish now") after the five-persona consultation recommended preparing it (WF-073).

- **Published.** Annotated tag `v1.11.1` on `d407039`; Release run `44268995907` **success**; release published `2026-11-18T08:58:35Z` with **39 assets**. Verified: the x86_64 Linux tarball sha256 matches its published `.sha256` (`ae0fd50a...`), `gh attestation verify` passes, and the release body is the `[1.11.1]` CHANGELOG section (MTU/EMSGSIZE, firewall, proxy filter, chrono -> time, M1, Actions pins, advisory trim). This is the first end-to-end publish since the hardened release workflow and the first run of the SHA-pinned `release.yml`.
- **Release-gate bug found by the tag.** `release-state` has no checkout and `gh release view` refuses outside a git repository, so `ready=false` and **every** packaging/channel job was skipped. Fixed: `GH_REPO` on the job, plus a `workflow_dispatch` `tag` + `run_bumps=true` mode that runs the same bump jobs for an already-published release (no build, no publish); every bump job now takes the tag from `release-state.outputs.tag` instead of `GITHUB_REF_NAME` (which is `master` on a dispatch). The read-only probe is skipped in bump mode.
- **Channels completed.** AUR PKGBUILD/`.SRCINFO` bumped to 1.11.1 with the published checksums and `publish-aur` published `lightspeed-bin 1.11.1-1`. Bump run `44272258596` completed every channel but its `Publishing channels self-test` failed because `test_publish_aur.sh` hard-coded 1.11.0; the test now derives the shipped version from `dist/aur` and uses the next minor for its bump fixtures. Corrected bump run `44272928238` is **fully green**. Final channel state: GitHub Releases 1.11.1; Homebrew formula 1.11.1; Chocolatey 1.11.1 pushed (HTTP 201, moderation queue); Scoop source 1.11.1 but bucket **not synced** (`SCOOP_TOKEN` missing, owner); AUR 1.11.1-1; winget bootstrap PR **microsoft/winget-pkgs#481953** still open (external).
- **Fleet rollout started.** At the release check the relays were mid-rollout: `relay-sgp-1` and `relay-syd-1` on 1.11.1 (uptime ~500 s), the other six still 1.11.0 on their original process generation; the hourly health-gated updater should finish all eight within ~1 h. M1 counters reset per relay on restart (skipped by the detector, as designed).
- **`#168` follow-up posted** (routine release notice, standing grant): https://github.com/ShibbityShwab/lightspeed/issues/168#issuecomment-6974090664 - asks the reporter to retest with `tunnel_mtu = 1400` and confirm the starving-return-path line is gone; no profile date promised.
- **Docs.** `docs/publishing-channels.md` refreshed to the 2026-11-18 state, including the bump-dispatch recovery mode.

**Verified:** release checksum + Sigstore attestation + changelog body; corrected bump run green on all channel jobs; channel versions read back from GitHub, the Homebrew formula, the Chocolatey nuspec, the Scoop manifest and the AUR RPC; evidence `wat/evidence/2026-11-18/release-prep/`. CI/CodeQL for the release commits are separate workflow runs (the tag run is the release proof).
**Open:** app.rs Phase A (next); fleet rollout completion; `SCOOP_TOKEN` (owner) and winget PR #481953 (external); field M1 trend and the `#168` retest; earlier backlog (registry re-sign not needed, #176/game requests, §5).

### 2026-11-18 (remediation) - WF-070: audit fixes landed (MTU/EMSGSIZE, Windows CI lint, firewall, IP filter, docs, deps)

**Agent:** lead. Owner instruction: "Nice work lets start tackling all this" after the WF-069 audit.

- **F-01 (the `#168` bug).** `[tunnel] mtu` / `--mtu` is now an honoured path-MTU cap: default 1492 (preserves the old clamp), range 1280-1500, on `TunnelConfig`/`PmtudConfig`/`PathMtuDiscovery` and threaded through `InterceptorConfig.path_mtu` (engine setter + `build_config_*` default), the Windows interceptor (`classify_at`, `send_datagram_at`) and `UdpRelay::with_pmtud`. On `EMSGSIZE`/`WSAEMSGSIZE` (10040) the interceptor now lowers the effective MTU by 64 bytes (floor 1280), retries the datagram with fragmentation, and records `send_errors`/`last_send_error`; `InterceptorCounters`, `InterceptorSnapshot`, `InterceptorHealth` and `EngineStatus` carry them, and the GUI's no-reply trace says a local send failure "cannot be the cause" and names the OS error. GUI `config.toml` gained `tunnel_mtu` (clamped; no UI field yet). Lifecycle: `client/src/tunnel/{pmtud,transport,relay}.rs`, `client/src/interceptor/{traits,windows}.rs`, `client/src/engine.rs`, `client/src/config.rs`, `client/src/cli.rs`, `client/src/main.rs`, `client/src/modes/intercept_mode.rs`, `client-gui/src/{config,app}.rs`.

### 2026-11-18 (hygiene) - Remote branch cleanup: 43 branches -> 2

**Agent:** lead, on owner request ("I see a lot of open or old branches are those necessary can we clean any orphaned ones up?").

- **Classification (43 remote branches).** 40 were head branches of merged PRs with zero unique work (39 ancestors of `master`; `dependabot/cargo/patch-ee65abdbca` was squash-merged as PR #174). One was `dependabot/cargo/eframe-0.37.0`, a closed unmerged branch (PR #158, 2026-10-22) whose bump had already landed on `master` as `d575d48` (`client-gui/Cargo.toml` is `eframe = { version = "0.37", ... }`), so its two commits are superseded. Two are genuinely long-lived and were kept: `master` (default) and `stats` (data-only orphan branch written hourly by `.github/workflows/pages.yml` and read by `health-anomaly.yml`; its tip moves constantly - `af97dac` at cleanup time). No PR was open and no workflow references any deleted branch name.
- **Action.** Deleted the 41 orphaned branches in one `git push origin --delete` (41/41 `[deleted]`, exit 0), then `git fetch --prune` dropped the local tracking refs. Enabled repository setting `delete_branch_on_merge` (`false -> true`) so merged PR head branches are removed automatically from now on - that setting being off is why ten weeks of merged branches had accumulated. `master` is unchanged at `bfd3b87`; the worktree is clean; only the WAT state files changed.
- **Verification.** GitHub branches API after: exactly 2 (`master bfd3b87`, `stats af97dac`); `git ls-remote --heads origin` lists exactly those two refs; local `git branch -r` shows only `origin/master` and `origin/stats`; `delete_branch_on_merge` reads `true`; the two non-ancestor heads stay recoverable as `refs/pull/158/head` (`bbb66d3`) and `refs/pull/174/head` (`b986acf`). Evidence: `wat/evidence/2026-11-18/branch-cleanup/` (`report.md`, `classification.txt`, `delete.log`, after-state files).

**Open:** none. `stats` must not be deleted: the Pages publisher writes it hourly and the anomaly monitor reads it.

  copyright and the site footer name the owner only; historical release

### 2026-11-17 (later) - Backlog follow-ups: publishing channels wired, registry signing fixed, images reviewed, WinDivert debt cleared

**Agent:** lead + `infra-dev-3` (publishing-channel wiring). Follows the audit round above; the owner replied to it and asked for five follow-ups.

- **Owner decision - no rollback; announcements become a rule.** The fleet was never rolled back: the owner accepted the update requirement ("this is a free service and I am fine with people needing to update"). The missing piece was communication, so this round added `[COMMS_STUB]` to `wat/rules.md` (every compatibility/user-visible change ships a Discussions -> Announcements post), a Release Notes "Announcements" step in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and the first such post: **[discussion #177](https://github.com/ShibbityShwab/lightspeed/discussions/177)** explains that 1.11.0+ is required on the community relays, how to update, and the self-hosted escape hatch. The GUI does prompt on launch (update check -> dialog, `client-gui/src/app.rs` `start_update_check`); users who never restart it will not see the prompt, which is why the announcement exists.
- **Publishing channels audited and repaired.** Root causes: the 1.11.0 release skipped every bump job (the 403), and three channels were never wired at all. Actions taken: **Chocolatey** 1.11.0 pushed through the existing `Choco Push` dispatch (run `44006128768`, `push HTTP 201`; moderated, feed still serves 1.10.3 until approved); **Scoop** bucket updated to 1.11.0 (`bucket/lightspeed.json` sha `d43c5b54`), and a gated `publish-scoop` job + hermetic suite now sync future tags (`SCOOP_TOKEN` is the one owner step); **winget** was never bootstrapped - the two first-version PRs (#435790 1.4.3, #437292 1.4.4) passed validation and sat unmerged until closed, the CLA was already signed and `WINGET_TOKEN` already exists - so the 1.11.0 manifests were refreshed to schema 1.12.0 and a replacement PR opened: **[microsoft/winget-pkgs#481953](https://github.com/microsoft/winget-pkgs/pull/481953)** (moderator review is the remaining external step; `winget-releaser` will publish future versions once it merges); **AUR** has no package at all (the name `lightspeed` is an unrelated app; `lightspeed-bin` is free) - `dist/aur` was bumped to 1.11.0-1 with the published sha256 values, a gated `publish-aur` job added, and the first push needs the owner's AUR account + SSH key (`AUR_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY`); **Homebrew** was already current (in-repo formula 1.11.0). Channel state/procedure: `docs/publishing-channels.md`.
- **Registry: no re-sign needed, tooling fixed instead.** The audit's F2 finding (stale `published_at`) was downgraded after re-reading the code: `published_at` is only a monotonic anti-rollback floor (no expiry), and nothing reads `issued_at`; the live document is authentic and breaking nothing. The tooling defect was real though: `Registry` did not model `issued_at` (any re-sign would silently drop it) and the signer copied timestamps verbatim. `sign_registry` now stamps `published_at = max(now, previous + 1)` and `issued_at = now` by default (`--keep-timestamps` for an unchanged re-sign), `Registry` preserves `issued_at`, two tests pin it, and the operator guide/audit record that signing is rare and deliberately offline - GitHub Pages hosts the file, nothing recurring needs the owner's key. Gate: client registry suite **23/0**, example compiles, fmt/clippy clean.
- **winget deprecation fixed.** The old 1.4.3 bootstrap PR was submitted on manifest schema `1.6.0` and carried `Manifest-Version-Deprecated`; the replacement 1.11.0 PR (#481953) was opened on `1.12.0` (no deprecation label), and on 2026-11-17 the three manifests were bumped again to **`1.28.0`** - the schema current merges use - with the same identifier/MSI/sha256, `winget validate --manifest` passing locally on winget 1.29.380, and the new head re-pushed to the PR branch (validation pipeline re-ran). Remove this as a concern unless the moderator reports something new.
- **AUR published (1.11.0-1).** Done end-to-end through the computer-use browser and the Windows git client: the AUR account key was registered via the account form (Chrome's password-manager autofill supplied the confirmation field; no password was typed or read by the agent), the profile save also required normalizing the Homepage field to `https://chrissinclairdesign.com` because AUR validates it as a full URL, and the first push went through (`ssh -T aur@aur.archlinux.org` works; commit `e690531`; PKGBUILD/.SRCINFO byte-identical to `dist/aur`). The package page now shows **lightspeed-bin 1.11.0-1**, submitter/maintainer `chrissinclair`, three v1.11.0 sources; `AUR_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` is stored as the repository secret, so `publish-aur` is armed for future tags. AUR's RPC/search index can lag a freshly created package (returns `resultcount: 0` while the page is live) - the verification docs check both.
- **WinDivert cleanup (task-11) closed.** With no `lightspeed-gui` process and no WinDivert service, an elevated one-liner removed the product's stale rules: **614 -> 543 total, `LightSpeed WinDivert Tunnel*` 71 -> 0** (script + log in `wat/evidence/2026-11-17/windivert-cleanup/`); firewall profiles, adapters and DNS untouched.
- **Reporter images actually reviewed.** All 7 image attachments across every issue (open and closed) were downloaded and viewed, and the follow-ups use them: **#148 Aion 2** is settled - the screenshots show the world connection is **TCP** (`ESTABLISHED ...:13328` plus TCP 443) with only a loopback UDP socket, so there is no remote UDP port to profile and the request is blocked on the TCP feature (#176), not on the reporter; **#168 Delta Force**'s screenshot is the in-game region selector (latencies, no endpoints), so the app-trace ask stands. Both issues got image-informed comments ([#148 comment](https://github.com/ShibbityShwab/lightspeed/issues/148#issuecomment-6936528459), [#168 comment](https://github.com/ShibbityShwab/lightspeed/issues/168#issuecomment-6936528994)); `docs/game-requests.md` rows updated.
- **CI follow-through on the new publishers.** The three new hermetic suites first executed in CI and found four real defects, fixed in `d6803f1`, `4d6d191`, `4548048`, `c8bff35`: the scripts were committed 100644, so direct-invocation usage probes exited 126; the Scoop stubbed `gh` never recorded the PUT message the assertions read; the Scoop script validated `GH_TOKEN` before `--bucket-repo` shape, so a malformed repo reported a missing token instead of the usage error; and the AUR publisher parsed `source_*` at column zero while makepkg's `.SRCINFO` is tab-indented, so it refused the shipped file. Final CI run `44010542448` on `c8bff35`: **14/14 jobs green**, including `Release publish self-test`, `Scoop publish self-test` and `AUR publish self-test`.

**Verified:** full local gate after the changes: fmt clean, workspace clippy clean (two pre-existing manifest warnings), **1,745 workspace tests / 0 failed / 7 ignored**, GUI **184/0/1**, GUI clippy clean (`wat/evidence/2026-11-17/lead-gate/final2.log`). CI run `44010542448` on `c8bff35`: **14/14 jobs green**, including the three publisher self-tests. Channel evidence in `wat/evidence/2026-11-17/channels/` (106/106 static checks, `winget validate` success, 3/3 tarball hashes for the AUR PKGBUILD, shellcheck 0 findings, YAML parse); Chocolatey push run `44006128768` 201; Scoop bucket readback 1.11.0; winget PR #481953 open and valid; registry tests 23/0; WinDivert before/after in the cleanup log.

**Open:** winget PR #481953 needs a winget-pkgs moderator (then `winget-releaser` takes over); AUR first push needs the owner's AUR account + `AUR_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` (and the PKGBUILD bump is still manual - a `bump-aur` job is the follow-up); Chocolatey 1.11.0 is awaiting moderation; the M1 drain (task-10) is unchanged; machine translations remain unreviewed.

- **Release pipeline root cause + fix (task-9, "403 on POST /releases").** A throwaway-branch probe (`workflow_dispatch`/push, `permissions: contents: write`, run `43989309532`) created a git ref and a draft release with the Actions token: **the 2026-11-16 403 is not reproducible** and the runner log then already showed `Contents: write`, so it was environmental (that window also hit an artifact-egress 503), not a persistent permission gap. The tag path is now hardened rather than re-permissioned: `infra/scripts/publish-release.sh` (idempotent create-or-`--clobber`, bounded 5xx/429/403 retry, actionable persistent-403 verdict, `--dry-run`, refuses `pull_request`), `release-state` gates Homebrew/Chocolatey/Scoop/winget/`verify-bumps` on the **published release with every expected asset** instead of `host` success, `workflow_dispatch` became a read-only probe, and `infra/scripts/test_release_publish.sh` (64 assertions, stateful `gh` stub) runs in `Release` on every event and in `Infra Script Tests`. Background and manual fallback documented in `docs/deploy-proxy.md`.
- **Release-pipeline verification (no tag needed).** `Release` dispatch run `43991546508` on `c837a84`: `plan`, `Release publish self-test` and `Release publish probe (read-only)` success, and every publish/bump/announce job skipped - a PR/dispatch cannot publish. CI run `43991534350` on the same head: **14/14 jobs green** including `Infra Script Tests`, whose new `Release publish self-test` step passed. The real tag path (create-or-clobber, bumps + `verify-bumps`, choco/winget) is proven only by the next release.
- **Health-monitor instrument (F6 follow-up, task-7/task-8).** The `saved_app` metric-name typo and the signed-delta rule had already been fixed in `76fb04b`; this round closed the remaining deploy-burst blind spot: the collector now treats a per-relay **version change** as a restart in addition to a probe-counter rollback (a young process replaced by one that already overtook its counters was invisible). +7 collector-suite assertions and +4 anomaly-suite assertions pinning the producer->detector negative-count chain; `Infra Script Tests` green.
- **Network audit (task-10 meter).** All 8 relays: `healthy`, `1.11.0`, `control_alpn=required`, same processes since the 2026-11-16 rollout restart; 742,116 packets / 133.4 MB relayed, 84 sessions, 55,097 drops (99.2% auth rejections), `rejected_other=0`. **M1 (`rejected_no_application_protocol`) is still increasing**: +390 rejects in 102.9 s (~13,650/h) with 0 accepted handshakes, and +227,826 rejects vs +946 accepted (~241:1) since the flip. The three "flat" relays had zero accepted handshakes in the window, so they are **not** evidence of a clean window. The drain is NOT complete: the `§7.4` replacement gate (accepted >= 200 and M1 == 0 across a multi-hour per-relay window) stays open, and no relay may be declared clean. Both `Deploy Proxy` runs triggered by the merges were version-keyed no-ops (fleet uptimes continuous, config unchanged).
- **Registry audit.** Published `registry.json` signature verifies (39/39 checks) against the compiled operator key and the payload is byte-identical to `web/registry.json`. `published_at` is older than the payload's `issued_at`, but that is cosmetic: `published_at` is a monotonic anti-rollback floor (not an expiry) and nothing reads `issued_at`; no re-sign is needed. The tooling gap is closed — `sign_registry` stamps both timestamps by default and `Registry` models `issued_at` so a re-sign cannot drop it (tests added).
- **GitHub backlog triaged (task-3).** 10 public comments (3 issues, 7 discussions), `blocked` label on #168, discussion #69 closed resolved; every action URL is in `docs/github-triage-2026-11-17.md`. `docs/game-requests.md`/`docs/supported-games.md` corrected: the TCP tunnel **is shipped** (protocol v5, Minecraft Java reference profile), the Tibia profile is UDP-only so it cannot accelerate Tibia, user/custom profiles still reject `tcp_ports`, and #176 is the tracker. No dates or compatibility promises.
